#2820. Mark Davis: "Absolutely no pressure whatsoever" from me to start Fernando Mendoza

The Raiders have gotten good play out of quarterback Kirk Cousins through the first eight games of the season, with the team sitting at 4-4 after Sunday’s victory over the Jets.

With Cousins keeping the team in contention, it’s unclear when No. 1 overall pick Fernando Mendoza might make his first career start.

That’s just fine with team owner Mark Davis, who talked about the young QB in a Monday interview with CNBC.

“From me, there’s absolutely no pressure whatsoever,” Davis said, via Adam Hill of the Las Vegas Review-Journal.

“Today, there’s a lot of pressure put on teams when they draft a guy No. 1 that you have to play him right away,” Davis added. “That’s why I think we were so fortunate to get Kirk Cousins as someone who could bridge the gap and let this young man learn a lot about what football is in the pros, the speed of the game, and the things that have to be done. … And he’s picking it up great, but I think we’re fortunate he doesn’t have to go out and play.”

Davis doesn’t want Mendoza to feel like he has to feel any kind of pressure to perform, because that could end up hindering his growth.

“I’ve seen quarterbacks get fast-tracked onto the field and they may do well, but they don’t learn all the intricacies of footwork and everything else it takes,” Davis said. “So they’re limited in their development, maybe throughout their career.

“I’m really happy with the way it is with Fernando right now, and we’ll see what happens.”

Much of Davis’ happiness with Mendoza has to do with how the QB has handled everything off the field.

“I tell people jokingly that if he can play football, he’s going to be amazing,” Davis said. “Because he’s got every other intangible you could want in a person. He’s a hard worker. He’s a sponge.

“[And] he’s working with Mr. [Tom] Brady on a lot of things.”

Mendoza has made one appearance so far this season, completing 6-of-8 passes for 48 yards in mop-up duty during the Week 5 loss to the Patriots.

#2815. Barcelona star dedicates interview to late Tenerife player after Champions League win

FC Barcelona forward Raphinha had a brilliant day on the field but struggled through the morning after the news of the death of Tenerife's Jesus de Miguel.

De Miguel, affectionally called "DeMi" by teammates and friends, died Tuesday, Nov. 3, the day after a harsh collision with an outrushing goalkeeper led to the player experiencing cardiac arrest.

Speaking to the CBS Sports post-match show featuring ex-players like Thierry Henry and Micah Richards, the Barcelona star emphasized that it was not the day to talk about what happened between the lines, even as he helped Barcelona to a 3-0 UEFA Champions League win over Aston Villa with a double.

"Today I'm very sad. I don't want to speak about football. I apologize to you because I'm joining you on the show and honestly I love to be here but I'd like to send strength to Jesus' family who today woke up to very sad news, the news of his passing. I'd like to say that wherever Jesus may be that he find his way," the forward said. "I think it's a much more difficult moment right now for his family.

"I just want to send them strength and leave soccer a bit to the side. Today, I'm not going to speak about football."

While few top players in Spain had played against De Miguel, Raphinha said he felt a strong since of solidarity with the late player's friends and family, partly because De Miguel was 30 when he passed, the same age as Raphinha and partly because of the bonds of being a professional player.

"For me, honestly, it was very difficult. I spoke with my wife today when I woke up and saw the news. Of course, I didn't feel good. I felt bad. He's a fellow professional and this could happen to anyone - to me, any player who was playing on the field today," he said. "He was a player who was the same age as me. I don't know if that is why it affected me more, but I didn't have a good day today because of that news. Personally, it really affected me."

FC Barcelona announced shortly after the news was published that it would conduct a moment silence before the match, which eventually expanded to time being taken before each UEFA Champions League contest.

De Miguel's body is being transferred to Madrid per his family's wishes with his Tenerife teammates attending services there before a celebration of his life Saturday, Nov. 7 at the club's home stadium.

#2812. Bills release S C.J. Gardner-Johnson, who just served 1-game suspension for spitting at Brock Bowers

The Buffalo Bills announced that they are releasing veteran safety C.J. Gardner-Johnson.

This was Gardner-Johnson's first season in Buffalo after spending last year with the Houston Texans and the Chicago Bears.

The safety played in six games for Buffalo. He tallied 26 combined tackles and one interception, which he returned for 41 yards.

Gardner-Johnson missed last week's game against Baltimore. He served a one-game suspension for spitting at Brock Bowers during the Bills' Week 6 win over the Las Vegas Raiders.

After his ejection, he posted an apology to his Instagram story.

"I want to take a moment to address my actions and sincerely apologize to my opponent, my teammates, my organization, and everyone who supports me.

"My passion and competitive nature have always been a huge part of who I am, but I also understand that there is a line that should never be crossed. The gesture I made toward my opponent was inappropriate, unnecessary, and not a reflection of the respect I have for him or this game.

"There is not excuse for my actions, and I take full accountability. No matter how emotional or intense the competition gets, I have to hold myself to a higher standard.

"To my opponent, I sincerely apologize. We compete hard between those lines, but respect should always remain at the forefront.

"One moment does not define my character, but how I respond to that moment matters. I will learn from this, grow from it, and continue to lead with the integrity, passion, and respect that have carried me throughout my career.

"I love this game, I respect this game, and I will always protect the integrity of this game.

"My actions will speak louder than my words moving forward."

The 28-year-old is now subject to waivers, but if he clears them, he will be a free agent and eligible to sign with any team.

Gardner-Johnson has been in the league since 2019, when the New Orleans Saints selected him in the fourth round of that year's NFL Draft. The safety spent three years with New Orleans, then bounced around the league, stopping in Philadelphia, Detroit, Houston and Chicago before landing in Buffalo this season.

Over his eight-year career, he has played in 93 games and recorded 21 interceptions, 396 tackles and 7 sacks.

#2811. Jerry Jones claims (incorrectly) that there's "no correlation" between coaching pay and winning

Jerry Jones has said that the size of the check he would write to guarantee another Super Bowl win would be “embarrassing.”

Jones, when it comes to the resources he is willing to invest in the team, is cheap. He won’t fire coaches who have buyouts. And he won’t pay top dollar for new coaches.

The owner and G.M. of the Cowboys defended his approach during his latest visit to 105.3 The Fan in Dallas by making the nonsensical claim that there’s no connection between coaching pay and winning.

“In the NFL, this is after years of looking at all the teams and all the coaches, there is no — there is zero correlation between how much you pay a coach, or how many coaches you have, as to winning,” Jones said. “There is no correlation. And that’s a random sample of years and all 32 teams involved.  So put that in your hopper and smoke it.”

He’s just wrong. Hiring a proven coach, and paying him accordingly, can change everything. We’ve seen it, time and again.

Andy Reid instantly turned the Chiefs into consistent contenders, and three-time Super Bowl winners. Sean Payton has made the Broncos into an annual contender, positioning him to become the first coach to win a Super Bowl for two different teams. Most recently, John Harbaugh has immediately changed everything for the Giants.

Harbaugh’s success will result in his former rival, Mike Tomlin, fending off teams willing to pay him upwards of $30 million per year to swap out his earpiece and microphone on the NBC sideline set for the Sony headset along the white stripe.

Jones prefers to speculate, when it comes to coaches. In lieu of paying for a coach who has proven that he can win, Jones wants to roll the dice on an unproven commodity in the hopes of maximizing the return on his investment.

“You can be pretty creative in hiring your coaches,” Jones said. “You can be creative sometime when you don’t even know you’re hiring the coaches.  And so I’ve had some good experience of hiring people who have never been in the NFL. I’ve had some experience of hiring young coaches, older coaches, and so, of course, you constantly remind me, as you should, of what my record is. And I understand that.  But I also know that the next time you throw the pick, if you were the old California gold miner, you may hit the Comstock Lode. And then it is gloryhole.”

“Creative” is another way of saying “cheap.” Buy low, and reap the benefit when the less expensive option yields results. And stick with him until his contract expires, to avoid having to pay two coaches at the same time.

It would be different if there truly was no correlation between quality (and cost) of coach and winning. But there ABSOLUTELY is a connection.

Still, Jerry prefers to dig through the bargain bin in search of an abandoned Botticelli. His own twist on “if you have to ask you can’t afford it” is “if you have to ask I don’t want it.”

It’s no surprise. In the early days of the salary cap, members of the media often pointed out that there’s no cap on coaching pay. In time, the NFL realized, “Oh shit, there’s no cap on coaching pay.”

That has led to what some believe is an effort to collectively keep coaching pay from growing at the rate that it should. And some in league circles are convinced that Jerry Jones is the ringleader of what is, as a practical matter, an effort to collude.

Even if he’s not going to pay top-of-market money for a coach, the higher the market goes, the more expensive it gets across the board. The rising tide, in other words, raises all dinghies. And Jerry would much rather spend his money on his aircraft carrier that has been retrofitted into a luxury hotel.

That’s his prerogative. But Cowboys fans — who fork over their money and who devote their time and attention to watching games on TV and consuming Cowboys content — should understand what’s going on. Jerry Jones is pinching pennies when it comes to paying coaches. As a result, he’s often getting what he isn’t paying for.

His mindset is highly relevant to the looming wreckage of the 2026 season. If coach Brian Schottenheimer has guaranteed money left on his contract for 2027, he will not be fired. And if Jones can avoid owing Schottenheimer a buyout, Jones will resist joining the pursuit of Tomlin, who admitted on the most recent edition of Football Night in America that he grew up a fan of the Cowboys.

Actually, that could make Jerry think that deep-seated rooting interest will give him a chance at hiring Tomlin on the cheap. Because cheap does as cheap is. And, when it comes to his team (and specifically to his coaches), Jerry Jones is cheap.

#2810. Merritt Island falls to Pine Crest in Class 3A state semifinals

Merritt Island traveled to Polk State College in Winter Have on Nov. 3 for the Class 3A state semifinals against Pine Crest.

The Panthers avenged their regular season defeat to the Mustangs with a four-set win 28-26, 18-25, 25-18, 25-21.

"Props to Pine Crest. They came out to win and they just played really well," Merritt Island head coach Angie Patrick said. "We had a hard time containing at the net, getting our first ball side out. This level it can go either way because all the teams are so good."

It was an even match statistically with Merritt Island spiking one more kill than Pine Crest, tallying two more assists and two fewer service errors. Pine Crest's biggest advantage was serve receive errors as Merritt Island finished with 10 and Pine Crest ended with four. The Panthers scored five more points than the Mustangs on errors.

Merritt Island rallied from a 17-13 deficit in set one and had set point at 25-24 and 26-25, but Pine Crest claimed the set with three straight points. The Mustangs rebounded in set two sprinting out to a 9-1 lead and sporting multiple leads of nine points.

After the Mustangs scored the first point of set three, Pine Crest rattled off four straight and never trailed the remainder of the set. The Panthers led wire-to-wire in set four and led by as many as eight points.

Mustangs junior Gena Walters led the team with 16 kills. Junior Ema Michell posted a double-double of 14 kills and 21 digs. Junior Lydia Scott finished with 12 kills and 13 digs.

In their final high school match, senior Amber Ives tallied 44 assists and senior Reece Logins recorded 18 digs.

"We just talked about how this game doesn't define the season they had. They had an amazing season," Patrick said. "We had an amazing regional. Just dominated. When you get on the state championship floor, you got to learn to deal with pressure situations."

Merritt Island finishes the season with a 25-7 record. It reached the final four in back-to-back seasons for the first time since 2011-12. The Mustangs graduate eight seniors in Sadie McClellan, Whitney Wisniewski, Khloe Horton, Alyssa Passmore, Ives, Loggins and Bella Becker.

"I just feel for our seniors," Patrick said. "They have given so much to the program. I thank them for everything that they've done. We'll just keep this fire in our hearts for next year."

FLORIDA TODAY's Craig Bailey contributed to this report.

This article originally appeared on Florida Today: Merritt Island falls to Pine Crest in Class 3A state semifinals

#2805. Jaguars place two starting defenders on injured reserve

The Jacksonville Jaguars have officially placed DT DaVon Hamilton and S Eric Murray on injured reserve.

On Monday, head coach Liam Coen said that both would be out for the season.

Hamilton exited Sunday's game against Indianapolis with what was later determined to be a quad injury. Murray left with an elbow injury.

Losing Hamilton creates a big void, both in his play and in the leadership he brings to the team.

Hamilton plays a vital role in the Jaguars' stingy run defense. Even on plays where he doesn't show up on the stat sheet, his ability to eat up space and take on blocks frees up other defenders to make plays.

In Hamilton's absence, the Jaguars will have to rely even more heavily on Arik Armstead, Ruke Orhorhoro, and rookie Albert Regis. Jacksonville also signed veteran Austin Johnson to the practice squad on Monday.

Without Murray, the Jaguars could turn to either Rayuan Lane or Kaevon Merriweather to start next to Antonio Johnson -- the other is likely the third safety in Anthony Campanile's defense. Jalen Huskey likely takes on a larger special teams role as a well as a result.

This season, Murray has made 14 total tackles and has held opponents to 6.0 yards per catch on only three receptions.

The Jaguars now have two openings on the 53-man roster to fill.

#2804. Sione Vaki emerges as Lions' change-of-pace back to pair with Jahmyr Gibbs

Even though the Lions have only played seven games and most teams have played eight, Lions running back Jahmyr Gibbs is leading the NFL with 191 touches — a huge workload that raises questions about whether Detroit needs a player to share carries with Gibbs, the way David Montgomery had done before he was traded to Houston.

Vaki had a career-high 11 carries and a career-high 65 yards in Sunday’s win over the Vikings, and his direct running style was a valuable change of pace for the Lions to have while Gibbs got some breathers between his own 23 carries for 153 yards.

Lions coach Dan Campbell emphasized after the game that the Lions told Vaki they needed him to step up against the Vikings, and Vaki answered the bell.

“Vaki was one of the guys we challenged this week going into this game,” Campbell said. “We were going to need him. He needed to show up. He was going to need to get some carries for us, some significant carries. They needed to be real yards, and he was going to need to be able to pass protect for us. And I thought he took a big step today. That’s a huge step. He gained a lot of trust today, a ton.”

Lions quarterback Jared Goff said the entire offense is excited about what Vaki can bring.

“Vaki ran his tail off,” Goff said. “And when he’s able to do that, it just gives Gibbs that much more of a breath on the sideline to come back and do his thing at his full capacity. And the O-line loved it. They loved the challenge of, like, we’re going to keep leaning on you guys. And I thought they played great.”

The Lions think they have the best running back in the league in Gibbs. If Vaki can prove to be one of the better backup running backs in the league, the Lions’ running game will be in very good shape.

#2802. Detroit Lions create 2 roster spots, place Jimmy Rolder on IR

On Tuesday, the Detroit Lions announced two roster moves, both creating vacant spots on the team's 53-man roster.

As expected, rookie linebacker Jimmy Rolder was placed on injured reserve after suffering what coach Dan Campbell called a season-ending injury. It was officially announced as a hamstring injury, and Campbell said surgery could be an option for the former Wolverine.

"Jimmy Rolder is going to require surgery, or he could. The point is this is a significant injury. He'll be out for the season," Campbell said on Monday.

It's a tough break for Rolder. He missed much of training camp with a hamstring injury, and Sunday was his first significant action on defense all year. In just 17 snaps, Rolder recorded five tackles, a sack, and another tackle for loss. Combined with the indefinite loss of Jack Campbell, the Lions are a bit shorthanded at linebacker.

The other move the Lions made was waiving rookie safety Bishop Fitzgerald. Undrafted in 2026, Fitzgerald was signed off the Steelers' practice squad when the Lions were particularly vulnerable at the safety position. But with Brian Branch returning recently—although in concussion protocol this week—and with Kerby Joseph returning soon, Fitzgerald may have just been the odd man out. It's worth noting that last week, Campbell indicated that Thomas Harper was nearing a return from injury, as well.

With those two moves, the Lions now have two vacancies on the 53-man roster. One of those spots is expected to be taken by linebacker K.J Britt. On Monday, a report dropped that the Lions were signing the veteran special teamer off the Patriots' practice squad, and Campbell confirmed it on his radio appearance on Tuesday.

At this point, it isn't clear what the Lions plan to do with the other open roster spot.

#2799. Bantams pierce Pirates' hull

Nov. 3—Lexi Roberts floated the ball to an inviting height near the center of the net, Laney Augir capitalized with a decisive leaping spike, and the Clarkston Bantams closed out the Rogers Pirates of Spokane in their 2A Greater Spokane League district volleyball tournament opener on Monday.

Though they had to work hard in the final set, the third-seeded Bantams came through with a sweep, prevailing 25-10, 25-11, 25-19 to please the home crowd at Clarkston High School.

"We played really good," said Clarkston setter Lexi Roberts, who racked up 27 assists — more than one for every three points won by her team. "We had a lot of energy, and we all connected really well."

The Bantams (11-4) advance to the semifinal round, where they will next take on second-seeded West Valley of Spokane Valley at 6 p.m. today on the Eagles' home court.

"We've just been working really hard on getting consistent defense and serve-receive, and we've been working really hard this season on being unpredictable with offense," Clarkston coach Hannah Winter said. "We really challenged the girls tonight to switch up their offense and try different things."

Joslyn McCormack-Marks had a complete showing for the Bantams, finishing with eight kills, four blocks and two aces. In a highlight moment for the junior middle blocker, she executed a relatively rare "slide" play, cutting out of her normal position to field one of Roberts' sets at the right-hand side of the net.

"It was pretty cool, because I don't normally do that," McCormack-Marks said. "It's hard to do."

Bailey Blaydes and Laela Leavitt led the back row effort for Clarkston, finishing with six kills apiece.

The outcome of each of the first two sets was never meaningfully in doubt, while an energized Rogers capitalized on a slight dip in Clarkston's level to lead early in the third before the Bantams righted the ship.

This was Clarkston's third victory over Rogers this season, all of them coming in straight sets. The result broke a two-match skid from the end of the regular season.

Clarkston has split previous meetings with West Valley, topping the Eagles in three sets on Oct. 1 but losing to them in four on Oct. 26. Both teams lost twice to Pullman and beat everyone else to finish the regular season with matching league records of 9-3; West Valley received the district tournament's second seed based on a preseason tiebreaking draw result.

Today's matchup is both a season rubber match and a chance for year-to-year redemption, as the Bantams were eliminated by the Eagles in last year's district semifinals. The winner will advance to the district final and secure a guaranteed berth to a District 5/6 crossover against opposition from the Central Washington Athletic Conference.

How will Clarkston go about extending its season?

"We really want to try and outsmart the Eagles, and keep the ball moving in different places so they don't know where it's going," Winter said.

McCormack-Marks added that the Bantams must "have a mentality of winning the whole time, and not get in our heads even when they score on us."

#2797. Jerry Jones wants to see more targets for George Pickens

The 3-5 Cowboys have plenty of problems. During the first of his two weekly radio spots on 105.3 The Fan in Dallas, owner and G.M. Jerry Jones mentioned one specific thing that he’d like to see change.

Regarding receiver George Pickens, Jones said this, via Jon Machota of The Athletic: “He needs the ball coming his way more.”

Through eight games, Pickens has 62 targets. In Week 8 against the Cardinals, Pickens had the ball thrown in his direction only four times. Against the Eagles in Week 7, Pickens had six targets.

He has 39 catches for 533 yards and two touchdowns in 2026. That projects to 83 catches for 1,132 yards and 8.5 touchdowns.

In 2025, Pickens finished with 93 receptions, 1,429 yards, and nine scores.

Hovering over the situation is the decision the Cowboys will have to make after the season. He’s making $27.298 million under the franchise tag; they could tag him again next year, at $32.758 million. Which is still nearly $10 million behind the top of the receiver market.

There’s another option, now that the Cowboys are fading in the race for the NFC East title. They could make Pickens available in trade.

Would a contender offer a second-round pick for him? The Chiefs are currently desperate for help at receiver; they’re interested (we’re told) in Jaguars receiver Brian Thomas Jr. and Colts receiver Josh Downs. If the Chiefs are in on Pickens, other teams in the AFC (like the Bills) should be thinking about it, too.

If the Cowboys were to decide to make a move, they could stockpile a pick or two — and save $15.17 million in cash and cap space. For now, who knows? The Cowboys seem to be deluding themselves into thinking they’re still contenders.

Unless that’s just part of the annual effort to keep their fans coughing up cash for tickets and merch. Which continues to work very well, relative to the on-field production.

#2793. DT Adetomiwa Adebawore agrees to two-year extension with Colts

The Colts took a break from working on trading away players ahead of next Tuesday’s deadline to assure that one member of the team will be sticking around for a while.

According to multiple reports, they have agreed to a two-year contract extension with defensive tackle Adetomiwa Adebawore. Per those reports, it is a $35 million deal with a $13 million signing bonus and includes $22.5 million through the 2027 season.

Adebawore was a 2023 fourth-round pick in Indianapolis and he matched his career high with four sacks through the first eight games of this season. He has 67 tackles, 10.5 sacks, 13 tackles for loss, and a fumble recovery over 51 career appearances.

With DeForest Buckner now on the 49ers, Adebawore is set for a bigger role on the Colts’ front and his new deal could prove to be a bargain if he builds on his strong start to the 2026 season.
